## Analysis Summary

### Comparison Snapshot

| Metric | Value |
|---|---|
| Runs covered | `94` |
| Scored events | `46` |
| Excluded `non-AV` events | `139` |
| Excluded `accidental` events | `360` |
| Overall success rate | `89.1% (41/46)` |
| `unpudo` success rate | `88.1% (37/42)` |
| `unparking` success rate | `100.0% (4/4)` |
| Ownership / handover failures | `0.0% (0/5)` |
| Short AV-attempt failures | `20.0% (1/5)` |
| Successful events with no driver accel help | `100.0% (41/41)` |
| Successful events with route change found | `82.9% (34/41)` |
| Successful gear alignment at anchor | `100.0% (41/41)` |

### Failure Profile

- The scored subset contains `5` failures out of `46` scored events. The dominant effective failure types are `failed_to_unpudo` 80.0% (4/5), `failed_to_slow` 20.0% (1/5).
- Ownership loss and handover breakage are the main theme: `0` of `5` failures fall into `not_av_owned`, `completed_outside_av`, `driver_completed_maneuver`, or `interrupted_handover`.
- Route-change evidence inside failed events is `found` in `5`, `not found` in `0`, and `unclear` in `0` cases.
- Source disengagement labels inside failed events are `failed_to_unpudo` 80.0% (4/5), `failed_to_slow` 20.0% (1/5); anything else is failing from DBW / ownership / gear evidence rather than an explicit source disengagement label.
- Event-type split: `unparking` is stronger than `unpudo` in this sample: 100.0% (4/4) versus 88.1% (37/42).

### Excluded Events

- `139` events are labelled `non-AV`: the detected maneuver starts outside AV ownership, so they are mentioned for coverage but excluded from model scoring.
- `360` events are labelled `accidental`: AV participates for less than `2s`, so they are treated as accidental contact rather than a meaningful model attempt.

### Success Behavior

- Successful events are distributed as `37` `unpudo` and `4` `unparking`.
- On successful attempts, route change is recovered in `34` of `41` cases and driver accelerator help is absent in `41` of `41` cases.
- Gear state stays aligned at the anchor in `41` of `41` comparable passes. Planned-indicator alignment is `26.8% (11/41)`, and controller-indicator alignment is `26.8% (11/41)`.
- Typical successful timing is median `AV -> event start` `43.80s`, median `AV -> first motion` `43.81s`, and median AV-owned duration `194.15s`.
- Typical successful maneuver shape: common actual gear at the anchor is `DRIVE_POSITION_V2_DRIVE`, common actual indicator is `INDICATORS_STATE_V2_OFF`, and median max AV speed is `6.29 m/s`.

### Written Assessment

This card covers `545` detected events across `94` runs, but only `46` of them are scored AV-owned attempts. The remaining `139` `non-AV` and `360` `accidental` events are explicitly excluded from the success-rate denominator. Within the scored subset, the overall success rate is `89.1% (41/46)`. `unparking` is stronger than `unpudo` in this sample: 100.0% (4/4) versus 88.1% (37/42).

Across the scored failures, route-change evidence is `5` found / `0` not found / `0` unclear, so navigation context is usually present. The failure mix is led by `failed_to_unpudo` 80.0% (4/5), `failed_to_slow` 20.0% (1/5), while the excluded portion is mostly `non-AV` starts or sub-`2s` accidental AV contact rather than true scored failures.

When this model succeeds, the behavior is consistent: route change is recovered in `34` of `41` successful events, driver accelerator help is absent in `41` of `41`, and gear alignment holds in `41` of `41` comparable passes. The typical successful event starts moving about `43.81s` after AV engagement, reaches a median max AV speed of `6.29 m/s`, and most often begins in gear `DRIVE_POSITION_V2_DRIVE` with indicator state `INDICATORS_STATE_V2_OFF`.
